### Optimizer strategies
We can implement several local methods:
1. Smooth softmax SLSQP (global-ish) using all lags but gradient can be computed via top weights maybe sparse. Need objective. Could compute weights exp(beta*(v-max)) for all lags; then gradient = dx * sum w_l * grad_l / sum_w. If weights dense, summing all lags O(n^2) Python but can maybe do in vectorized? For n up to 512, n^2 loops could be okay for a few evals, but SLSQP many. Could restrict to top W weights (e.g., 128) with renormalized weights; approximate gradient. Or compute exact full gradient for moderate n if number of SLSQP iterations small (<=30) and active lags weights top 96. Since high beta concentrates, top weights enough. Use previous smooth objective but with vectorized optim? Could use active-set instead.

2. Active-constraint min-t SLSQP: choose top k lags, solve min t with constraints f_l <= t. SLSQP uses Jacobian m x (n+1). For n=320,k=64, 320x64 constraints, feasible. Objective t. ### Use `cvxpy` to solve moment / SOCP relaxation? Let's explore if possible to get upper bound by constructing h from relaxation? Maybe not.

The objective over h is max of bilinear. We can introduce variable p_{i,i+L}=h_i(1-h_{i+L}). Since h_i- h_i h_{i+L}. Constraints p <= h_i, p <=1-h_{i+L}, p >= h_i - h_{i+L}? For bilinear equality? If we solve linear maximization of p? We need minimize t with p_l = sum. Linearizing p = h_i - h_i h_{i+L}. For fixed h_i? It's rank-1. But perhaps a linear upper bound: h_i(1-h_j) <=? We can use McCormick envelopes to create convex/concave relaxations if we fix signs. h_i(1-h_i-h_j)?? For minimizing max, using convex overestimator maybe. Could solve min t with t >= sum of convex upper bounds? If we find feasible h and t from relaxation, t is upper bound? Let's see: For 0<=h_i,h_j<=1, h_i(1-h_j) is bilinear. Its minimum over square is 0, maximum 1. McCormick: w = h_i - h_i h_j. Envelopes: w >= h_i - h_i*h_j? That's itself. We need upper bound on w: w <= h_i, w <= 1-h_j, w <= h_i + 1 - h_j -? The smallest upper bound linear is w <= min(h_i, 1-h_j). Sum of min cannot linearize easily but can use w <= (h_i +1-h_j)/2? Not tight. Solve linear min t with w variables and w <= h_i, w <= 1-h_j, t >= ∑w, 0<=h<=1. This is LP: Then w can be 0 if both constraints slack, so t minimal? Since minimize t and w appear positive in t constraints, choose w=0, t=0. Need lower? Not useful. For minimizing max of product, need lower bounds? Actually product is what we minimize, cannot upper-bound; need exact. If we solve with w >= conv envelope lower, minimizing t will choose low w maybe infeasible actual. So no.

Could use semidefinite: let X = h h^T. For each lag, f = sum h_i - ∑ X_{i,i+L}. Max t. Constraints X PSD, X_ii=h_i, 0<=h<=1. Min t. This is SDP relaxation. It gives lower bound on optimum? `project_feasible`: As previous but faster. Need ensure exact sum.
Could implement more efficient: given h,n:
- clip nonfinite
- enforce bounds? Use binary search threshold tau for sum after clipping. Then adjust.
Binary search 60 iterations enough. Then adjust diff over non-bound; to make sum exact, use one/two elements.
Pseudo:
```
def project_feasible(h,n=None):
    h=np.asarray(h,float).copy()
    replace nonfinite with 0.5
    if n is None: n=h.size
    target=0.5*n
    if n<=0: return empty
    if h.size<n pad zeros else truncate
    h=np.clip(h,0,1)
    # If sum already target, return (maybe exact within)
    # find tau via binary: sum(clipped h-tau)
    lo=min(h)-1; hi=max(h)+1
    for 80:
      mid=(lo+hi)/2; s=sum(np.clip(h-mid,0,1))
      if s>target: lo=mid
      else: hi=mid
    tau=(lo+hi)/2
    out=np.clip(h-tau,0,1)
    # iterative distribute diff
    for _ in range(50):
      s=sum(out); diff=target-s
      if abs(diff)<1e-15: break
      if diff>0: mask=out<1-1e-15
      else mask=out>1e-15
      cnt=count(mask)
      if cnt==0: break
      add=diff/cnt
      # if add too large for bound? but tau should feasible; still clip
      out[mask]+=add
      out=np.clip(out,0,1)
    # exact adjust one
    diff=target-sum(out)
    if diff !=0 and out.size>0:
       if diff>0:
          idx=int(np.argmin(out))
       else:
          idx=int(np.argmax(out))
       val=out[idx]
       add=diff
       if idx==0? ensure bounds. If val+add <0 or >1, choose another or distribute?
       if 0 <= val+add <=1:
          out[idx]+=add
       else:
          # distribute to many bounds
          ...
    return out
```
This should be fine. But binary search on array each iteration O(n*80). For n up to 512, okay. Could optimize by using stable? Fine.

Note: For parent h maybe already feasible with tiny error. project may slightly change c5. Need preserve exact parent. Previous code if raw parent feasible within tol, uses raw without projection. We'll do: if parent has sum close to n/2 and bounds, keep raw for initial best, but for optimization project copies. For final, project best but if it degrades? Project should maintain feasible, may change objective slightly. If parent raw has exact sum, projecting could alter due tau=0? If h already feasible, binary search tau=0? If sum=target, s=target at tau=0, binary may end tau ~0. But out=clip(h,0,1) same, then diff exact maybe. It may not degrade. But floating clipping might no change. Fine. But to be safe, at start if parent feasible, best_h=parent copy, best_c=compute_c5(parent). For future, use project_feasible for candidates; if projected c slightly worse due numerical but okay.

`compute_c5`: Use `float(np.max(np.correlate(h,1-h,mode="full"))* (2/n))`. If n=0 return 0. Ensure 1-h vector. For large n, np.correlate maybe slices? okay.

`all_lag_values(h)`: Use autocorr.
```
def all_lag_values(h):
 n=len(h); dx=2/n
 pref=np.empty(n+1); pref[0]=0; np.cumsum(h, out=pref[1:]); total=pref[-1]
 auto=np.correlate(h,h,mode='full') # length 2n-1
 center=n-1
 vals=np.empty(2*n-1); lags=np.empty(2*n-1,dtype=int)
 ac0=auto[center]
 vals[0]=(total-ac0)*dx; lags[0]=0
 idx=1
 for L in range(1,n):
   ac=auto[center+L]
   m=n-L
   vals[idx]=(pref[m]-ac)*dx; lags[idx]=L; idx+=1
   vals[idx]=(total-pref[L]-ac)*dx; lags[idx]=-L; idx+=1
 return vals,lags
```
Need check `auto` symmetric: np.correlate(h,h) yes. For n=1 length1.

Maybe direct `np.correlate(h,1-h)` for c5; all_lag_values might have small differences but max same.

`choose_active_lags(vals,lags,max_count,margin)`: sort descending, top max_count plus near top margin? Return unique sorted by value. Need cap maybe max_count*3.

`lag_value(h, L)` as previous maybe for constraints. Use dot. For speed in SLSQP constraint eval, we can compute active values via dot; but we need also t - value. For Jacobian, lag_gradient.

`lag_gradient(h,L)` as previous.
`add_lag_gradient_to(g,h,L,coef)` maybe careful sign. We'll directly compute full gradient in functions.

`smooth_h_objective(x)`:

Implement:
```
def smooth_h_jac(x):
 h=x; n=len(h); beta=SM_BETA
 vals,lags=all_lag_values(h)
 maxv=vals.max()
 d=beta*(vals-maxv)
 w=np.where(d>-745, np.exp(d),0.0)
 totalw=sum(w)
 if totalw==0: # use argmax gradient
   L=lags[argmax(vals)]; g=lag_gradient(h,L); return dx*g
 g=np.zeros(n)
 dx=2/n
 for i,L in enumerate(lags):
   c=w[i]*dx/totalw
   if c>1e-300:
      add_lag_gradient_to(g,h,int(L),c)
 return g
```
`add_lag_gradient_to` adds coef*grad of lag L (already scaled without dx? lag_gradient returns derivative of f_L value? Previous lag_gradient for f_L (not multiplied dx). In smooth_jac, multiply dx. Good.)
Loop over all lags 2n-1 (e.g., 511 for n=256). Each add does two slice adds. That's fine. For n=1024 ~2047 loops, maybe okay if few. Use top weights threshold to skip tiny: if c < 1e-12*maxw? Could skip. We can skip if w[i] < 1e-12*totalw. Good.

`smooth_h_objective(x)`: compute vals all_lag, return maxv + log(sum exp)/beta. Use all_lag O(np.correlate). Good.

But SLSQP with n=256, each objective calls np.correlate(h,h) O(n^2) C and all_lag loop 256; gradient similarly. Number of objective/gradient calls maybe. Could be okay. Profile? SLSQP 40 iterations, each maybe 2 func + 2 jac => maybe 160 evals. np.correlate n=256 maybe ~? 256^2=65k mult, C <0.1ms? Actually maybe 0.3ms. 160 =>50ms. Python loops for jac 511 slice ops *160=80k slice ops, maybe 0.1s. Fine. For n=512, np.correlate 262k mult, 1ms, loop 1023 slice ops *160=160k, okay. ### Local search

### More mathematical insights for improvements

Could exploit symmetry: The objective values for L and -L not symmetric due boundary. But the problem over x in [0,2] maybe symmetric under x -> 2-x and k -> -k? If h mirrored, lags swap. We can enforce? Not necessary.

C for 1-h equals max over k of ∫(1-h(x))h(x+k) = max negative/lag? Since full set includes both, C(1-h) = C(h)? Because values for h include ∫h(1-h shifted); for 1-h include ∫(1-h)h shifted = ∫h shifted (1-h). The set over all shifts maybe same? For finite, maybe yes if all lags. Thus complement symmetry. We can use candidate 1-h? But sum of 1-h over length2: integral=1? Wait ∫0^2 1-h = 2 - ∫h =1, so yes feasible. Discrete sum: sum(1-h)=n - n/2 = n/2. So complement is feasible! Objective likely same? Let's test: corr(1-h, h) vs corr(h,1-h). np.correlate(1-h,h) = reverse? The max full should equal maybe same set shifted? Since a,b swapped; correlation of a,b full is reverse of correlate(b,a)? ### Time budgeting implementation

Pseudo `run`:
```
def run(seed=42,budget_s=1300,**kwargs):
  seed=int(seed); budget_s=float(budget_s); rng=RandomState(seed)
  t0=time.time(); end=t0+budget_s-25
  parent=get_parent_values()
  if parent is not None:
    pn=len(parent)
    raw_feasible = bounds and abs(sum-target)<=1e-8
    if raw_feasible: best_h=parent.copy(); best_c=compute_c5(best_h)
    else: best_h=project_feasible(parent,pn); best_c=compute_c5
    best_n=pn
  else: best_n=160; best_h=...
  best_c = max(best_c,0)
  # function improve_n(h0,n,time_limit,seed,include_smooth,include_active,include_local)
  # Stage 1: parent n
  if time<end-60:
    inits = build_inits_for_n(best_n, parent, best_h, best_n, rng, count=4)
    hh,cc=process_inits(inits,best_n,time.time()+180,seed)

  # Stage 2: low ns maybe 128,144? Not needed but can find lower.

  # Stage 3: high ns: [int(best_n*1.2),192,224,256,288,320]
  process each with slots depending on n.
  # Final polish best.
```

```
def balance_exact(h):
  n=h.size; target=0.5*n
  out=np.clip(h,0,1)
  diff=target - out.sum()
  if diff ==0: return out
  # Do up to 8 iterations: add diff/cnt to allowed mask
  for _ in range(8):
    if diff>0:
      mask=out < 1.0 - 1e-15
      slack=None
    else:
      mask=out > 1e-15
    cnt=mask.sum()
    if cnt==0: break
    add=diff/cnt
    # To avoid overshoot, if add too large for min slack, clip by reducing add? But diff should small.
    # Compute max allowed add for positive: min(1-out[mask]) maybe. If add > max_allow, set add=max_allow*0.99 and let next iterations.
    if diff>0:
       max_add = (1.0-out[mask]).min()
       if add > max_add: add=max_add*0.98
    else:
       max_add = out[mask].min()
       if -add > max_add: add=-max_add*0.98
    out[mask]+=add
    out=np.clip(out,0,1)
    diff=target-out.sum()
    if abs(diff)<1e-15: break
  # final exact adjust one
  diff=target-out.sum()
  if abs(diff)>0:
     if diff>0:
        idx=np.argmin(1-out)
     else: idx=np.argmax(out)
     # choose index with slack
     if diff>0:
       idx=np.argmax(1-out)
       add=min(diff, 1-out[idx])
     else:
       idx=np.argmin(out)
       add=max(diff, -out[idx])
     out[idx]+=add
     diff=target-out.sum()
     if diff !=0:
        # distribute among top 3 maybe
  # Hard clip
  return np.clip(out,0,1)
```
But `balance_exact` with iterative may not produce exact due after final one perhaps diff not 0 due rounding. Could then adjust another with `out[k] = target - sum(out without k)` for a safe k. But not necessary if tol.

```
def pair_delta_move(h,i,j,step,rng):
  a=h[i]; b=h[j]
  d=rng.uniform(step, step*3) * (1 if rng.rand()<0.5 else -1)
  # maybe target move from high to low? Just random
  # limit to keep within [0,1]
  if a + d <0: d = -a
  if a + d >1: d = 1-a
  if b - d <0: d = b
  # Also if b-d>1? since subtract, if b-d >1 when d negative: d = b-1
```

For Q, terms involving t:

Simpler: compute new terms involving i and j and subtract old terms involving the set of terms affected.
Affected terms:
- For each t in {i,j}, term t,t+L if valid, term t-L,t if valid. There may be duplicates if i and j are L apart (same term). Let set of term start p = t or t-L. For L>0, term (p,p+L).
Old Q_aff = sum h[p]*h[p+L]. New Q_aff = sum (h[p]+δ_p)*(h[p+L]+δ_{p+L}) where δ_t is d1/d2 for i/j else 0.
ΔQ = new_aff - old_aff. This handles cross.
Thus per L, we can identify up to 4 term starts. For each term, compute old/new using h before and δs. O(1).
For L=0, affected term (i,i), (j,j). ΔQ = (h_i+d1)^2 - h_i^2 + (h_j+d2)^2 - h_j^2.

Local pair moves implementation:
```
def local_pair_search(h0,n,time_limit,seed):
```

### Pseudo local search
```
def local_pair_search(h0,n,time_limit,seed):
  base=project_feasible(h0,n); best=compute_c5(base); cur=base.copy(); ccur=best
  rng=RandomState(seed)
  temp=0.02; min_temp=1e-6; trial=0
  while time<limit and trial<max_trials:
    r=rng.rand()
    if r<0.35:
      # block swap
      max_len=min(8,n)
      L=int(rng.randint(1,max_len+1))
      # choose two intervals non-overlap
      for _ in range(5):
        a=int(rng.randint(0,n-L)); b=int(rng.randint(0,n-L)); if abs(a-b)>=L: break
      if abs(a-b)<L: continue
      h_try=cur.copy()
      h_try[a:b? Actually Python slice inverted... need temp copy
      tempv=cur[a:a+L].copy(); h_try[a:a+L]=cur[b:b+L]; h_try[b:b+L]=tempv
    else:
      # pair delta
      i=int(rng.randint(0,n)); j=int(rng.randint(0,n)); if i==j retry
      a=cur[i]; b=cur[j]
      lo=max(-a, b-1.0); hi=min(1.0-a, b)
      if hi-lo < 1e-12: continue
      # step scale
      scale = min(temp? maybe 0.05)
      d = rng.uniform(-scale, scale)
      if d<lo: d=lo+rng.rand()*(hi-lo)*0.5?
      elif d>hi: d=hi-rng.rand()*(hi-lo)*0.5
      h_try=cur.copy(); h_try[i]=a+d; h_try[j]=b-d
    c_try=compute_c5(h_try)
    if c_try<best-1e-15:
      best=h_try.copy? Careful: h_try maybe view? Use np.copy.
      cur=h_try.copy(); ccur=c_try; temp=min(0.08, temp*1.1)
    elif c_try<cur_c:
      cur=h_try.copy(); ccur=c_try
    else:
      dd=c_try-cur_c
      if dd<temp:
        if rng.rand()<np.exp(-dd/temp): cur=h_try.copy(); ccur=c_try
    temp=max(temp*0.997,min_temp)
    trial+=1
  return best,best_c
```

Implement `project_block_values(x, bs)` returns block out length B.
```
def project_block_values(x, bs):
  # x length B
  B=len(x); target=0.5*B
  h=np.clip(x,0,1)
  lo=min(h)-1; hi=max(h)+1
  for 60: mid... s=sum(clip(h-mid,0,1)); if s>target lo=mid else hi=mid
  tau=(lo+hi)/2; out=clip(h-tau,0,1)
  # iterative distribute over B
  for ... diff=target-sum(out); mask nonbound; add diff/cnt; clip
  return out
```
Then expand. This for DE and block starts.

For DE objective, use `block_eval`:
```
xb = project_block_values(np.clip(x,0,1), DE_N//DE_BS)
h=np.repeat(xb, DE_BS)
return compute_c5(h)
```
This enforces block pattern exactly sum? target B/2 times bs = n/2. Good.

This is much faster.

Maybe use DE to optimize **block variables directly** at coarse n (e.g., n=64 or 128) then upsample. But objective for block pattern at coarse n approximates. We can run DE at resolution n_parent perhaps 160 with bs=2/4. It may find block-constant patterns. Then sample refine.

Add `run_block_de(n, bs, time_limit, seed, maxiter=35, popsize=15)` with time check? DE doesn't accept timeout; maxiter bound. Choose maxiter based: if n>200 maxiter=25. Slots ~60s.

Could be too long but okay.

### Direct global random search + SLSQP
DE may find independent pattern. Include for selected n (160,192,256) only if enough time.

### Implement details carefully

#### project_feasible
```
def project_feasible(h, n=None):
    h = np.asarray(h, dtype=float).copy()
    h = np.where(np.isfinite(h), h, 0.5)
    if n is None:
        n = int(h.size)
    else:
        n = int(n)
    if n <= 0:
        return np.empty(0, dtype=float)
    if h.size < n:
       h = np.concatenate((h, np.zeros(n-h.size)))
    elif h.size > n:
       h = h[:n]
    h = np.clip(h,0.0,1.0)
    target=0.5*float(n)
    lo=float(np.min(h))-1.0
    hi=float(np.max(h))+1.0
    for _ in range(70):
       mid=0.5*(lo+hi)
       s=float(np.sum(np.clip(h-mid,0.0,1.0)))
       if s > target:
           lo=mid
       else:
           hi=mid
    tau=0.5*(lo+hi)
    out=np.clip(h-tau,0.0,1.0)
    # iterative
    for _ in range(20):
       diff=target-float(np.sum(out))
       if abs(diff) < 1e-14: break
       if diff > 0:
          mask=out < 1.0 - 1e-14
       else:
          mask=out > 1e-14
       cnt=int(np.count_nonzero(mask))
       if cnt==0:
          break
       add=diff/float(cnt)
       out[mask]+=add
       out=np.clip(out,0.0,1.0)
       diff=target-float(np.sum(out))
       if abs(diff)<1e-15:
          out[np.where(mask)[0]? no]...
    # final exact-ish
    diff=target-float(np.sum(out))
    if abs(diff)>1e-15 and out.size>0:
       if diff >0:
          idx=int(np.argmax(1.0-out))  # most slack
          add=min(diff, 1.0-out[idx])
       else:
          idx=int(np.argmin(out))
          add=max(diff, -out[idx])
       out[idx]=float(out[idx])+add
       diff=target-float(np.sum(out))
       if abs(diff)>1e-15:
          # distribute over top slack again
          if diff>0: mask=out<1-1e-15
          else: mask=out>1e-15
          cnt=max(1,int(np.count_nonzero(mask)))
          add=diff/float(cnt)
          out[mask]+=add
          out=np.clip(out,0.0,1.0)
    return out
```
Using `np.argmax(1-out)` for diff>0, `argmin(out)` for diff<0. Good.

If many values at bound and diff not small, iterative may not fix but rare.

#### project_block_values
Similar but B maybe small. Need if B<=0. `target=0.5*B`. After binary, if out sum target? Since B may odd target .5, binary works. Distribute over B. Then return.

#### compute_c5
```
def compute_c5(h):
 h=np.asarray(h,float)
 n=h.size
 if n<=0: return 0.0
 dx=2.0/float(n)
 corr=np.correlate(h, 1.0-h, mode='full')
 return float(np.max(corr)*dx)
```
If h has tiny out of bounds? Use clip? For evaluation, maybe clip to be safe: h=np.clip(h,0,1). But clipping could change objective to lower than returned h? We return projected h. In local moves h feasible. For safety, compute_c5 clips? If harness evaluates returned h, it expects returned h objective; if our compute clips, could return lower than true if h out of bounds. We can ensure returned in bounds. For internal, `compute_c5` can clip to avoid tiny negative causing weird. But if we clip, the objective of h (unclipped) maybe different; we always want exact. Use `h = np.asarray(h, float); if tiny: np.clip?` I think do not clip for c5 to match evaluator. But if values -1e-16, 1-h gives 1+1e-16; max may change tiny. No harm. For constraints, SLSQP enforces. For project output, values [0,1]. We'll not clip in compute, except maybe `h = np.where(np.isfinite(h), h, 0.5)`. Good.

#### all_lag_values
Use `auto = np.correlate(h, h, mode="full")`. If n=1, center 0. For L loop. Need `pref[m]` where m=n-L. `pref` length n+1. Good.

#### choose_active_lags
```
def choose_active_lags(vals,lags,max_count,margin=2e-5):
 if vals.size==0: return []
 order=np.argsort(-vals)
 take=min(int(max_count), vals.size)
 idx=order[:take]
 if margin>0:
   top=vals[order[0]]
   near=np.where(vals >= top - margin)[0]
   idx=np.unique(np.concatenate((idx,near)))
   idx=idx[np.argsort(-vals[idx])]
   cap=max(int(max_count)*2, int(vals.size))
   # But for n=320, cap could 640; too many. Cap min(cap, int(max_count)*3, 220?)
   cap = min(int(np.size(vals)), max(int(max_count)*2, 64), 240)
   if idx.size > cap: idx=idx[:cap]
 return [int(lags[i]) for i in idx]
```
Margin 2e-5 absolute C units. Could include many if plateau. Cap 240. SLSQP constraints 240 heavy. Maybe cap 128 for n>160. Use:
```
if n>200: cap=max(max_count*2,80); cap=min(cap,128)
else cap=max(max_count*2,120)
```
But choose doesn't know n. Pass max_cap parameter? We can add optional `max_cap=160`.
Simplify: cap = min(int(vals.size), max(int(max_count)*2, 64), 160). Good.

#### lag_gradient
Be careful with L=0 returns derivative of f_0 = sum h - dot hh: derivative 1 - 2h. For positive:
```
g=np.zeros(n)
m=n-L
low=h[:m]; high=h[L:]
g[:m] += 1.0 - high
g[L:] += -low
```
For negative:
```
g[L:] += 1.0 - low
g[:m] += -high
```
Good.

`add_lag_gradient` similar but coefficient. For L=0, g += coef*(1-2h) maybe. Need coefficient already includes dx/total? In smooth_jac, we call add with coef = w*inv*dx, and it should add coef * lag_gradient. For L=0 okay.
```
def add_lag_gradient(g,h,L,coef):
 if coef==0: return
 n=h.size
 if L==0:
   g += coef*(1.0-2.0*h); return
 L=abs(int(L))
 m=n-L
 if L>0 sign? We pass signed L. If signed>=0:
   g[:m] += coef*(1.0-h[L:])
   g[L:] += -coef*h[:m]
 else:
   g[L:] += coef*(1.0-h[:m])
   g[:m] += -coef*h[L:]
```
Good. In smooth_jac, loop `for i in range(len(lags)): coef=w*inv*dx; add_lag_gradient(g,h,int(lags[i]),coef)`.

#### active ineq

`active_ineq_jac`: rows. `J[i,:n] = -dx*lag_gradient(h,L)`, `J[i,n]=1`. For L=0 works.

Potential performance: Jacobian call recomputes lag_gradient for each active lag; okay.

#### optimize_smooth_h

```
def optimize_smooth_h(h0,n,beta,maxiter=45,ftol=1e-10):
 global SM_BETA
 h=project_feasible(h0,n)
 SM_BETA=max(1.0,beta)
 bounds=[(0.0,1.0) for _ in range(n)]
 constraints=[{"type":"eq","fun":smooth_sum_eq,"jac":smooth_sum_eq_jac}]
 try:
   res=minimize(smooth_h_objective,h,method='SLSQP',bounds=bounds,constraints=constraints,options={'maxiter':int(maxiter),'ftol':float(ftol),'disp':False})
   if res.x is not None ...
      h2=project_feasible(res.x,n); c2=compute_c5(h2); return h2,c2
 except Exception:
   pass
 return h,compute_c5(h)
```
Could if res.fun < original, use; else still project. But if SLSQP fails to improve, returning h2 maybe worse; caller handles.

```
def optimize_active_t(h0,active_lags,t0,maxiter=45,ftol=1e-10):

```

#### refine_active
```
def refine_active(h0,n,time_limit,active_seq,maxiter=45,margin=2e-5):
 h=project_feasible(h0,n); c=compute; best=h,c
 for acnt in active_seq:
   passes = 2 if acnt<=24 else 1
   for pp in range(passes):
     if time.time() > time_limit - 2.0: break
     vals,lags=all_lag_values(h)
     current=float(np.max(vals)) if vals.size else 0
     if current < best_c: best_c=current; best_h=h.copy()
     active=choose_active_lags(vals,lags,acnt,margin)
     if not active: active=[int(lags[int(np.argmax(vals))])]
     # maybe remove lags with tiny weight? no
     t0=min(1.0, current + 1e-5)
     res=optimize_active_t(h,active,t0,maxiter=maxiter,ftol=1e-10)
     if res is not None and res.x is not None and res.x.size==n+1 and np.all(np.isfinite(res.x)):
        h2=project_feasible(res.x[:n],n); c2=compute_c5(h2)
        if c2 < best_c: best_h/h2; best_c=c2
        if c2 <= current + 1e-8:
           h=h2
     # if no improvement two passes? break maybe
 return best_h,best_c
```
Potential active set includes many lags with top margin; if current best improved outside, next loop recomputes. Good.

```
def build_inits_for_n(n,parent,best_h,best_n,rng,low_sols,include_structured=False,limit=4):
 inits=[]

   src = interpolate_to_n(parent,n) if len(parent)!=n else parent.copy()
   inits.append(project_feasible(src,n))

   src2=interpolate_to_n(best_h,n) if best_n!=n else best_h.copy()
   add_unique(inits,project_feasible(src2,n))
 # variants from first src
 if inits:
   vs=variant_starts(inits[0],n,rng,count=3)
   for v in vs: add_unique(inits,project_feasible(v,n))
 for hs,cs,ns in low_sols[:1]: add interp
 if include_structured:
   st=structured_starts(n,rng,count=3)
   for s in st: add_unique
 return inits[:limit]
```

```
def polish_candidate(h0,n,slot,seed,do_local=False):
 h=project_feasible(h0,n); c=compute_c5(h); best_h=h.copy(); best_c=c
 if 2<=n<=320 and time.time()<slot-20:
   betas=smooth_betas_for_n(n)
   maxit=45 if n>220 else 55
   for beta in betas:
      if time.time()>slot-15: break
      h2,c2=optimize_smooth_h(h,n,beta,max iter=maxit,ftol=1e-10)
      if c2 < best_c:
         best_h=h2.copy(); best_c=c2
         h=h2.copy()
      elif c2 <= best_c + 1e-5: # near, use trajectory? maybe if c2 <= h current +? Need compute original c?
         h=h2.copy()
      else:
         # keep h as previous if not too bad? use best?
         h=best_h.copy()
 if 2<=n<=360 and time.time()<slot-10:
   seq=active_seq_for_n(n)
   maxit=45 if n>220 else 55
   h2,c2=refine_active(best_h,n,slot,seq,maxiter=maxit,margin=3e-5)
   if c2<best_c: best_h=h2; best_c=c2
 if do_local and time.time()<slot-5:
   h2,c2=local_pair_search(best_h,n,slot,seed+12345)
   if c2<best_c: best_h=h2; best_c=c2
 return best_h,best_c
```

### Mathematical possibility with high n and block patterns
If we can find binary h at n=192 with max count <=36, C=0.375. Is that feasible? Average f over all lags (sum/fvalues) lower bound average count ~3N/16? For N=192 avg count ~36. ### Binary local search objective and average
For binary h with ones count N/2, objective C = dx max f_count. We can run simulated annealing with swaps (1 with 0) to minimize max. This is different from continuous. Starting patterns: Thue-Morse, random, block, Beatty. Simulated annealing can optimize max. For N=256, number ones128, choose two indices (one 1, one 0) -> ~16k possible; many trials. Full eval 256. Let's design `binary_anneal(h0,n,time_limit,seed)`:
- h initial binary exact: take top n/2 values from h0? Or construct binary start.
- maintain list of one_indices and zero_indices for O(logN) swaps.
- For each move: choose random one i and zero j, swap. Evaluate C with np.correlate. Accept/reject.
- To speed, we could maintain `c` and use full compute each trial. N=256, 50k trials maybe 5-20s. Good.
- Temperature: objective values quantized. If C step = dx, temp start dx*5 maybe (0.004 for 256) and decay. Actually C differences often dx=0.0078. Use temp=0.02 initially, min 1e-6.
- Best track.
- Could also use **NewMark**: after accepting, maybe continue.

```
def binary_anneal_start(h0,n,time_limit,seed):
  h=make_binary(h0,n)
  ones=np.where(h==1)[0]; zeros=np.where(h==0)[0]
  best=compute; cur=h; ccur=best
  temp=0.02; min_temp=1e-7
  rng=RandomState(seed)
  while time<limit:
    i=ones[rng.randint(len(ones))]; j=zeros[rng.randint(len(zeros))]
    if i==j continue
    h[i]=1-h[i]? Let's swap: val_i=1,val_j=0 => after swap h[i]=0,h[j]=1
    h[i]=0; h[j]=1; ones list update: remove i add j; zeros remove j add i. Need O(n) if list pop/append; okay for n=256, 50k =12M. Could use arrays? Simpler with sets? List remove O(n). Use `ones = list(np.where...)` initially; on swap:
       try ones.remove(i); zeros.remove(j) O(n)
       ones.append(j); zeros.append(i)
     Or use indices arrays and swap positions by maintaining position arrays for O(1). Implement with lists and pos arrays.
```

```
def binary_starts(n,rng,count=4):

  # random binary
  b=make_binary(rng.rand(n),n)
  add
  return out[:count]
```

```
def process_n(n,slot,seed,parent,best_h,best_n,low_sols,do_local=False,limit=3,do_binary=False):
  rng=np.random.RandomState(seed)
  t_end=time.time()+slot
  inits=[]
  if do_binary and 60<=n<=320:
     bin_end = time.time() + min(slot*0.45, 90.0)
     bin_starts = binary_starts(n,rng,2)
     best_bin=None
     for b in bin_starts:
        if time>bin_end break
        bb,cc=binary_anneal(b,n,bin_end,seed+997)
        if best_bin is None or cc<best_bin_c: best_bin=bb; best_bin_c=cc
     if best_bin: add_unique(inits,best_bin,0)
  # add parent/best/variants
  other_inits=build_inits_for_n(...)
  for o in other_inits: add_unique
  inits=inits[:limit+1]
  intra_end = t_end - (20 if do_local else 2)
  for each init: per=... polish_candidate(no local)
  if do_local: local
```

Implementation `binary_update_vals(vals, h, i, j, d_i, d_j, n)`: update vals in-place for all lags given current h (before change) and deltas. For binary swap, d_i=-1 if i 1->0, d_j=+1 if j 0->1.
General formula for signed L:
- f0 = ∑h - ∑h^2. For binary, ∑h constant (since swap) so A0 constant 0? Actually A0=sum h_i, unchanged. Q0=∑h_i^2=number of ones = n/2, unchanged. f0=0 always for binary! Indeed for binary, k=0 overlap zero. So L=0 remains 0.
For L>0:
 Positive fL = A_pos - Q, A_pos=sum_{p=0}^{n-L-1} h_p.
 Negative fL = A_neg - Q, A_neg=sum_{p=L}^{n-1} h_p, Q=sum_{p=0}^{n-L-1} h_p h_{p+L}.
For swap with deltas d_i,d_j (sum 0), ΔA_pos = d_i if i<n-L else 0 + d_j if j<n-L else 0.
ΔA_neg = d_i if i>=L else0 + d_j if j>=L else0.
ΔQ for unsigned L: Sum over pairs p,p+L. Affected pairs involving i or j. For each affected start p, old product = h[p]*h[p+L]; new with deltas at i,j. Since d sum zero but products. Could compute candidate terms:
```
delta_q=0.0
# for t in (i with d_t, j with d_t): for p=t if t<n-L; p=t-L if t>=L:
# But if same p duplicate, avoid double.
```
Only 4 pairs. For each unique p:
```
hp=h[p]; hq=h[p+L]
# new values:
npv=hp + (d_i if p==i else 0) + (d_j if p==j else 0)
nqv=hq + (d_i if p+L==i else 0) + (d_j if p+L==j else 0)
delta_q += (npv*nqv - hp*hq)
```
Since H small, can use small list, but per L overhead. We can optimize by branch.
For each L, to avoid list, do:
```
dq=0
# pair i,i+L
if i < n-L:
  p=i; q=i+L
  old=h[p]*h[q]
  newp = h[p] + (d_i if p==i else 0) + (d_j if p==j else0) # p==i always unless j==i no

  dq += newp*newq - old
  # But if same pair also generated from j, avoid? We'll handle by only adding terms for starts in set.
```

Alternative: Compute ΔQ directly using derivatives: For bilinear Q = ∑ h_p h_{p+L}. Change at i by d_i, j by d_j. If i and j not partners, ΔQ = d_i * sum_{partners of i} partner_old + d_j * sum_{partners of j} partner_old. If they are partners, add d_i*d_j. Partners of i: i+L if exists, i-L if exists. Partners of j similarly. If i and j are partners, the cross term appears once. This is O(1) without unique starts.
Let's derive:
Old Q = ∑ h_p h_{p+L}. Let contributions involving index r: S_r = sum of h_r * h_partner over distinct partners p (i+L if valid, i-L if valid). If we change only r by d_r, ΔQ = d_r * partner_sum_r. If change two indices i,j: If i and j are not in same term (i.e., abs(i-j) != L), then ΔQ = d_i*P_i + d_j*P_j, where P_i = (h_{i+L} if valid)+(h_{i-L} if valid). If they are in same term, say j=i+L. Then the shared term h_i h_j changes by d_i h_j + d_j h_i + d_i d_j (using old h). The formula d_i*P_i + d_j*P_j would include shared term twice? Let's check P_i includes h_j; P_j includes h_i. Thus very simple!

```
  # Pi, Pj

  if i >= L: Pi += h[i-L]
  Pj = 0.0
  if j < m: Pj += h[j+L]
  if j >= L: Pj += h[j-L]
  dq = d_i*Pi + d_j*Pj
  if abs(i-j) == L: dq += d_i*d_j
  # dA pos
  da = 0.0
  if i < m: da += d_i
  if j < m: da += d_j
  if da != 0 or dq != 0: pos[L] += (da - dq)*dx
  # dA neg
  dan = 0.0
  if i >= L: dan += d_i
  if j >= L: dan += d_j
  if dan !=0 or dq !=0: neg[L] += (dan - dq)*dx
```

Let's implement helper:
```
def recompute_posneg(h,n):
 dx=2.0/n
 pref=np.empty(n+1); pref[0]=0; np.cumsum(h,out=pref[1:]); total=pref[-1]
 auto=np.correlate(h,h,mode='full'); center=n-1
 pos=np.zeros(n); neg=np.zeros(n)
 ac0=auto[center]; v0=(total-ac0)*dx; pos[0]=v0; neg[0]=v0
 for L in range(1,n):
   ac=auto[center+L]
   m=n-L
   pos[L]=(pref[m]-ac)*dx
   neg[L]=(total-pref[L]-ac)*dx
 return pos,neg
```
For binary h, L0=0 but okay.


### Binary anneal with posneg update
Pseudo:
```
def binary_anneal(h0,n,time_limit,seed):
 h=make_binary(h0,n)
 pos,neg=recompute_posneg(h,n)
 ones=list(np.where(h==1)[0]); zeros=list(np.where(h==0)[0])
 pos1=np.full(n,-1,lshape?); pos0...
 for idx,val in enumerate(ones): pos1[val]=idx; zeros...
 best_c=max(max(pos,max(neg))); best_h=h.copy()
 ccur=best_c
 rng=RandomState(seed)
 temp=0.03; min_temp=1e-7
 trial=0; recompute_counter=0
 while time.time() < time_limit:
   if not ones or not zeros: break
   i=ones[rng.randint(len(ones))]; j=zeros[rng.randint(len(zeros))]
   if i==j: continue
   # update posneg for new state
   update_binary_posneg(pos,neg,h,i,j,-1.0,1.0,n)
   c_try = max(float(np.max(pos)), float(np.max(neg)))
   if c_try + 1e-15 < best_c:
      # accept and apply h
      apply swap h/lists
      cur_c=c_try
      best_h=h.copy(); best_c=c_try
      temp=max(temp*1.05, min(0.05,?))? Maybe keep/ increase slightly
   elif c_try < ccur:
      apply swap; cur_c=c_try
   else:
      dd=c_try-ccur
      if dd < temp and rng.rand() < np.exp(-dd/temp):
         accept; cur_c=c_try
      else:
         update_binary_posneg(pos,neg,h,i,j,1.0,-1.0,n)
   temp=max(temp*0.997,min_temp)
   trial+=1; recompute_counter+=1
   if (trial & 1023)==0:
       pos,neg=recompute_posneg(h,n)
       ccur=compute_c5(h) # or max
       if ccur < best_c: update? h is current; best_h should be min; If ccur<best update best.
 return best_h,best_c
```
Need apply swap function top-level? To avoid nested, define `apply_binary_swap(h, ones, zeros, pos1, pos0, i, j)`? But modifying lists. Could implement inline twice. For clarity, top-level:
```
def swap_binary_state(h, ones, zeros, p1, p0, i, j):
    # i one -> zero, j zero -> one
    p=p1[i]; q=p0[j]
    ones[p]=j; p1[j]=p; p1[i]=-1
    zeros[q]=i; p0[i]=q; p0[j]=-1
    h[i]=0.0; h[j]=1.0
```
Assumes i in ones, j in zeros. Good.

Recompute current C: Since posneg maybe accurate, `ccur = max(max(pos),max(neg))`. But after recompute, use that. Update best if c new current lower? We already update best on improve. However if current moved to worse than best, best_h unchanged. If recompute finds current c lower due error, update.

Temperature: Objective step maybe dx; for n=192 dx=0.0104, dx*3=0.03. Start temp 0.05. Accept worse up to 0.05 common. Good.

Will binary anneal find low C? It starts from structured, operates on exact binary. It can escape local via accept worse. Good.

After binary anneal, continuous SLSQP can further reduce. But binary may be enough to beat target if low.

### Continuous local pair search can also use posneg update? For continuous pair delta not binary, f0 changes; formula similar with general deltas and Q linear + cross. We could implement generic `update_general_posneg` for continuous pair moves and use in local search, much faster than full correlate. Let's see. For general δ_i, δ_j (sum zero), f0 = sum h - ∑h^2; ΔA0=δ_i+δ_j=0; ΔQ0=(h_i+d_i)^2 - h_i^2 + (h_j+d_j)^2 - h_j^2. L=0 may change! For binary f0=0. For continuous local, need update L=0 too. Formula with partner sums:
For L>0 same ΔQ = d_i*P_i + d_j*P_j + cross if partners (cross=d_i*d_j). This formula works for general. ΔA edges. For L=0: Q0=∑h^2. ΔQ0=d_i*(2h_i)+d_j*(2h_j)+d_i^2+d_j^2. A0 constant if sum zero. f0 = A0 - Q0. So Δf0 = -ΔQ0. We can update pos[0]=neg[0] accordingly. This could accelerate continuous local search massively. Implement `update_pair_posneg(pos,neg,h,i,j,di,dj,n)` general. Then local search pair delta can use O(n). Block swap not incremental; can use full compute occasionally or only pair moves. Pair moves enough for local polish.

Let's implement generic update for both binary and continuous (binary special di=-1,dj=1). Then local pair search can maintain pos/neg and use O(n) updates, no full correlate each trial. This is powerful and different. Need ensure f0 tracking. For continuous, h not binary, L0 value may nonzero but usually small. It affects max? Could be. Update exactly.

General formula for L>0:
- Q_L = ∑_{p=0}^{n-L-1} h_p h_{p+L}
- P_i(old) = (h_{i+L} if i<n-L) + (h_{i-L} if i>=L)
- P_j similarly.
- ΔQ = d_i*P_i + d_j*P_j + (d_i*d_j if abs(i-j)==L else 0). This is exact for changes at two indices (bilinear). Check if i and j are partners: linear terms include shared product twice? Wait earlier for shared term, linear part d_i h_j + d_j h_i using old; plus cross. If not shared, linear terms from their respective partner sums. Good.
- ΔA_pos = d_i if i<n-L else0 + d_j if j<n-L else0. f_l = A - Q, so Δf = ΔA - ΔQ.
- ΔA_neg = d_i if i>=L else0 + d_j if j>=L else0.
L=0:
- f0 = ∑h_i - ∑h_i^2. A change sum zero => ΔA=0. ΔQ = 2h_i d_i + d_i^2 + 2h_j d_j + d_j^2. If i=j not allowed; if i,j same? no.
- Δf0 = -ΔQ. pos[0]=neg[0]+=Δf0.

This is O(n) Python. Then local pair search can be fast for n up to 512, many trials.

Need recompute periodically to avoid drift.

Implement `update_pair_posneg(pos, neg, h, i, j, di, dj, n)` top-level general. Use floats.

Potential issue if di,dj large near bounds. We choose d within allowed, so values remain [0,1]. Formula exact for quadratic.

For continuous local search, we can do random pair delta and **pair swap**? Pair swap general values: δ_i = h_j - h_i, δ_j = h_i - h_j, if within bounds (always) and sum zero. This is like transfer slack. Use generic update with di,dj. Swap two arbitrary values (not necessarily 0/1). It can improve structure. For binary, swap is a special di=old_j-old_i. But binary anneal uses only 1->0. We can use generic local for all.

Local search with posneg:
- Maintain h, pos,neg current.
- `c = max(pos.max(), neg.max())` (L0 included).
- Trial: choose move:
  - pair delta: random i,j, choose d within allowed interval scaled step; di=d, dj=-d.
  - value swap: choose i,j, di = h[j]-h[i], dj = h[i]-h[j]. (This is like moving values). Allowed automatically. If di=0 skip.
- Apply update to pos/neg using old h; c_try = max. If accept, apply h[i]+=di; h[j]+=dj. If reject, update with -di,-dj using old h (h unchanged) to revert.
- Periodically recompute.
- Also block swap? Could skip; pair moves enough. Maybe add occasional **three-index**? no.

This local search can run many trials (hundreds thousands) in budget. It can polish beyond 1e-4. Good.

Let's define `local_pair_posneg_search(h0,n,time_limit,seed)`:
```
h=project_feasible(h0,n)
pos,neg=recompute_posneg(h,n)
best_h=h.copy(); best_c=max(...)
ccur=best_c
temp=0.002; move_scale=0.05; min_temp=1e-8; min_scale=0.001
rng=RandomState(seed)
counter=0
while time<limit:
  t=rng.rand()
  if t<0.5:
    i,j random distinct
    a=h[i]; b=h[j]
    lo=max(0-a, b-1)  # since d for i; b-d in [0,1] => b-1 <= d <= b, a+d in [0,1] => -a <= d <=1-a
    hi=min(1-a, b)
    if hi-lo <= 1e-14: continue
    # choose d = rng.uniform(-scale, scale) clipped; if near 0 maybe sample within interval proportional
    d=rng.uniform(-move_scale, move_scale)
    if d < lo: d = lo + (hi-lo)*rng.rand()*0.5
    elif d > hi: d = hi - (hi-lo)*rng.rand()*0.5
    if abs(d) < 1e-12: continue
    di=d; dj=-d
  else:
    i,j random
    di=h[j]-h[i]; dj=-di
    if abs(di)<1e-13: continue
    # swap sometimes? This transfer moves i toward j. Could also use random scale? fine.
  # diagonal bounds? For delta move hi/lo ensure. For swap exact.
  update_pair_posneg(pos,neg,h,i,j,di,dj,n)
  c_try=max(np.max(pos),np.max(neg))
  if c_try + 1e-15 < best_c:
     h[i]+=di; h[j]+=dj; ccur=c_try; best=h.copy(); best_c=c_try
     temp=min(0.01, temp*1.1)
  elif c_try < ccur:
     h[i]+=di; h[j]+=dj; ccur=c_try
  else:
     dd=c_try-ccur
     if dd < temp and rng.rand() < np.exp(-dd/temp):
        h[i]+=di; h[j]+=dj; ccur=c_try
     else:
        # revert pos using old h (h unchanged)
        update_pair_posneg(pos,neg,h,i,j,-di,-dj,n)
  temp=max(temp*0.997,min_temp); move_scale=max(min_scale, move_scale*0.998)
  counter++
  if counter & 1023 ==0:
     pos,neg=recompute_posneg(h,n); ccur=max; if ccur<best update
return best_h,best_c
```
Need for swap move: If we set di=h[j]-h[i], dj=-di, h i becomes j's old value, h j becomes i's old value. Since h unchanged during update, update using old values and di exact; after accept, values swapped. Good. If reject, revert pos with -di.

But for swap move, if i and j values maybe equal skip. Also if we choose random i,j and swap, this is a large move; temperature accepts rare. Good.

```
def update_pair_posneg_vec(pos,neg,h,i,j,di,dj,n):
  m=n-1
  if m==0: return
  L = 1..m maybe not store as array.
  # DQ array length m initialized 0
  dq = np.zeros(m) # allocation heavy
  # contributions from i:
  # right: L=1..n-i-1 (length nr=n-i-1): partner h[i+1 : i+nr]
  nr = n - i - 1
  if di !=0 and nr>0: dq[:nr] += di * h[i+1:i+1+nr]? Wait P_i is partner sum, DQ includes di*P_i. So dq[:nr] += di * h[i+1:i+nr+1]
  # left: L=1..i (length il=i): partner h[i-1], h[i-2],..., h[0]
  il = i
  if di !=0 and il>0: dq[:il] += di * h[i-1::-1]  # length il from i-1 down to 0
  # similarly j
  if dj !=0: ...
  diff=abs(i-j)
  if diff>0 and diff<n: dq[diff-1] += di*dj
  # DA pos:
  dap = np.zeros(m) # allocation
  if di !=0: dap[:n-i-1] += di
  if dj !=0: dap[:n-j-1] += dj
  pos[1:] += (dap - dq)*dx
  # DA neg:
  dan = np.zeros(m)
  if di !=0: dan[:i] += di
  if dj !=0: dan[:j] += dj
  neg[1:] += (dan - dq)*dx
  # L0
  if di !=0 or dj !=0:
     dq0 = di*(2h[i]+di) + dj*(2h[j]+dj)  # if i!=j; if same? no
     pos[0] -= dq0*dx? Wait f0 = A - Q, sample f0 = sumh - sumh^2; C0 = dx*(sumh-sumh^2). If sum unchanged, Δf0 = -ΔQ0. Q0 sumh^2 sample units. ΔQ0 as above. So pos[0] += -dq0*dx.
```

`update_pair_posneg`:
```
def update_pair_posneg(pos,neg,h,i,j,di,dj,n):
    m = n-1
    if m <= 0:
       # L0 only
       if di != 0: dq0 = di*(2*h[i]+di)
       elif dj !=0: ...
       if i!=j: dq0 += ... else ...
       pos[0] -= dq0*(2/n); neg[0]=pos[0]
       return
    ensure_pq_work(n)
    dq=_PQ_DQ; dap=_PQ_DAP; dan=_PQ_DAN; t1=_PQ_T1; t2=_PQ_T2
    # only first m
    dq[:m].fill(0.0); dap[:m].fill(0.0); dan[:m].fill(0.0)
    if di != 0.0:
       nr = n - i - 1
       if nr > 0:
          dq[:nr] += di * h[i+1:i+1+nr]
          dap[:nr] += di
       il = i
       if il > 0:
          dq[:il] += di * h[i-1::-1]
          dan[:il] += di
    if dj != 0.0:
       nr = n - j - 1
       if nr > 0:
          dq[:nr] += dj * h[j+1:j+1+nr]
          dap[:nr] += dj
       il = j
       if il > 0:
          dq[:il] += dj * h[j-1::-1]
          dan[:il] += dj
    adiff = abs(i-j)
    if 0 < adiff < n:
       dq[adiff-1] += di*dj
    dx=2.0/float(n)
    np.subtract(dap[:m], dq[:m], out=t1[:m])
    t1[:m] *= dx
    pos[1:] += t1[:m]
    np.subtract(dan[:m], dq[:m], out=t2[:m])
    t2[:m] *= dx
    neg[1:] += t2[:m]
    # L0
    dq0=0.0
    if di != 0.0: dq0 += di*(2.0*h[i]+di)
    if dj != 0.0: dq0 += dj*(2.0*h[j]+dj)
    pos[0] -= dq0*dx
    neg[0] = pos[0]
```
Need consider if i==j; we won't call. If i and j distinct but di+dj=0, sum unchanged. If i&j affect L0 diagonals separate; no cross. Good.

But for L>0, if i and j are the same partner? We handled cross if adiff==L. The linear terms `di*h_partner` from slices use old h for partners including each other. For shared pair, P_i includes h_j old, P_j includes h_i old; slices add di*h_j + dj*h_i; cross adds di*dj. Correct.

Potential floating drift: reverse update might not exactly restore; periodic recompute.

#### `recompute_posneg`
Use `pref` and auto. Could avoid `all_lag_values` for local. Need return pos/neg length n.
```
def recompute_posneg(h,n=None):
 h=np.asarray(h,float)
 if n is None: n=h.size
 dx=2/n
 pref=np.empty(n+1); pref[0]=0; np.cumsum(h,out=pref[1:]); total=pref[-1]
 auto=np.correlate(h,h,mode='full'); center=n-1
 pos=np.zeros(n); neg=np.zeros(n)
 ac0=float(auto[center])
 v0=(total-ac0)*dx
 pos[0]=v0; neg[0]=v0
 for L in range(1,n):
   ac=float(auto[center+L])
   m=n-L
   pos[L]=(pref[m]-ac)*dx
   neg[L]=(total-pref[L]-ac)*dx
 return pos,neg
```
If n=0? handle.

```
def current_c_from_posneg(pos,neg):

```

#### `local_pair_posneg_search`
Need `ensure_pq_work` before loop? Inside update.
```
def local_pair_posneg_search(h0,n,time_limit,seed,temp0=0.002,scale0=0.05):
 h=project_feasible(h0,n)
 if n<=1: return h,compute_c5(h)
 pos,neg=recompute_posneg(h,n)
 best_h=h.copy(); best_c=fast_max
 cur_c=best_c
 temp=temp0; scale=scale0; min_temp=1e-7; min_scale=1e-3
 rng=np.random.RandomState(int(seed))
 trial=0
 while time.time() < time_limit:
   move_type = rng.rand()
   if move_type < 0.55:
      i=int(rng.randint(n)); j=int(rng.randint(n)); if i==j continue
      a=h[i]; b=h[j]
      lo=max(-a, b-1.0); hi=min(1.0-a, b)
      if hi-lo <= 1e-15: continue
      dlo=max(lo, -scale); dhi=min(hi, scale)
      if dlo > dhi:
         # choose feasible near boundary
         if rng.rand()<0.5: d=lo + 0.2*(hi-lo)
         else: d=hi - 0.2*(hi-lo)
      else:
         d=rng.uniform(dlo,dhi)
      if abs(d) < 1e-13: continue
      di=d; dj=-d
   else:
      i=...; j=...
      if i==j continue
      a=h[i]; b=h[j]
      diff=b-a
      if abs(diff) < 1e-13: continue
      # choose alpha
      r=rng.rand()
      if r<0.25: alpha=1.0
      elif r<0.75: alpha=0.3
      else: alpha=0.1
      di=diff*alpha; dj=-di
   update_pair_posneg(pos,neg,h,i,j,di,dj,n)
   c_try=fast_max(pos,neg)
   accept=False
   if c_try < best_c - 1e-15:
      accept=True; best_h=h.copy(); best_c=c_try; temp=min(0.008, temp*1.08)
   elif c_try < cur_c:
      accept=True
   else:
      dd=c_try-cur_c
      if dd < temp:
         # guard exp
         if dd < 0 or np.exp(-dd/temp) > rng.rand(): accept=True
   if accept:
      h[i] += di; h[j] += dj
      # clip tiny
      if h[i] < 0: h[i]=0
      elif h[i] >1: h[i]=1
      if h[j] <0: ...
      cur_c=c_try
   else:
      update_pair_posneg(pos,neg,h,i,j,-di,-dj,n)
   temp=max(temp*0.998, min_temp)
   scale=max(min_scale, scale*0.999)
   trial +=1
   if (trial & 2047)==0:
       pos,neg=recompute_posneg(h,n)
       cnow=fast_max(pos,neg)
       cur_c=cnow
       if cnow < best_c:
           best_h=h.copy(); best_c=cnow
       # optional reset if very bad
       if cur_c > best_c + 0.01:
           h=best_h.copy(); pos,neg=recompute_posneg(h,n); cur_c=best_c
 return best_h,best_c
```
Need if `h[i]` after di may be slightly out due floating; clip. But clipping changes sum and posneg! For feasible d, should be within except rounding. We can not clip or if clip occurs, need recompute? To be safe, if clipping by >1e-12, set `h[i]=min(1,max(0,...))` and then maybe recompute immediately and cur_c=fast_max. But rare. We can avoid by choosing d with 1e-12 margin in lo/hi: `lo=max(-a, b-1)+1e-12; hi=min(1-a,b)-1e-12`. For swap exact, safe. Use margin maybe 1e-12. If interval becomes empty skip.
In partial swap, di within allowed because moving toward each other. With margin maybe okay. Use epsilon 1e-12.
No need clip except tiny.

`np.exp(-dd/temp)` if dd/temp > 745 -> 0. Guard.

`temp` and `scale` floats; if `best_c` maybe 0, temp? ok.

```
   if processed skip
   slot = (140 if nn<=220 else 180 if nn<=280 else 120)*slot_scale
   slot = min(slot, main_end-now)

   do_local = nn in (best_n,192,224,256) and slot>80
```

### Final output bound
Return `float(best_c)` computed after final project. If final project c differs from tracked best_c by >1e-8, return project c. That is exact. If project c > current target, but tracked lower? We cannot report lower than actual. So return projected c. To minimize degradation, use `fast_balance` rather than full project? Let's implement `finalize_feasible(h,n)`:
- out=h.copy(); out=np.where(finite,out,0.5); out=clip
- diff=target-sum(out)
- If abs(diff)<1e-12 return out (already feasible) without shifting shape. Only adjust one/two elements.
This will preserve c better. Implement separate from `project_feasible`. Use final only.
```
def finalize_feasible(h,n):
 out=np.asarray(h,float).copy(); out=np.where(isfinite,out,0.5); out=np.clip(out,0,1)
 target=0.5*n; diff=target-out.sum()
 if abs(diff)<1e-13: return out
 # If diff positive, choose index with max 1-out, add min(diff, slack); if not enough, distribute
 for _ in range(10):
   if diff>0:
     idx=int(np.argmax(1-out)); slack=1-out[idx]
   else: idx=int(np.argmin(out)); slack=out[idx]
   if diff>0: add=min(diff, slack)
   else: add=max(diff, -slack)
   if add==0: break
   out[idx]+=add
   diff=target-out.sum()
   if abs(diff)<1e-14: break
 return np.clip(out,0,1)
```
This adjusts minimal. If diff large due initial infeasible, use project_feasible. We can call project first? In run, best_h always from project; diff small. Use `finalize_feasible` final. But SLSQP h maybe sum residual 1e-9; finalize good.

Need ensure sum exact in task. Floating exact maybe not; but tolerance likely.

```
def build_binary_index(h):
 n=h.size; ones=[]; zeros=[]; p1=np.full(n,-1,int); p0=np.full(n,-1,int)
 for i,v in enumerate(h):
   if v>0.5:
      p1[i]=len(ones); ones.append(i)
   else:
      p0[i]=len(zeros); zeros.append(i)
 return ones,zeros,p1,p0
```

```
def polish_candidate(h0,n,slot,seed,do_local=False):
 h=project_feasible(h0,n); c=compute_c5(h); best=h.copy(); best_c=c
 if 2<=n<=320 and time.time()<slot-25:
   betas=smooth_betas_for_n(n); maxit=...
   for beta in betas:
     if time.time()>slot-20: break
     h2,c2=optimize_smooth_h(h,n,beta,maxiter=maxit,ftol=1e-10)
     if c2 < best_c:
       best_h=h2.copy(); best_c=c2; h=h2.copy()
     elif c2 <= compute_c5(h)+1e-6? Need current h c? track cur_c.
   # active
 if 2<=n<=360 and time.time()<slot-10:
   seq=active_seq_for_n(n); maxit=...
   h2,c2=refine_active(best_h,n,slot,seq,maxiter)
   update
 if do_local and time.time()<slot-2:
   h2,c2=local_pair_posneg_search(best_h,n,slot,seed+777)

```

`process_n`:
```
def process_n(n, slot, seed, parent, best_h, best_n, do_binary=False, do_local=False, limit=2):
 rng=np.random.RandomState(int(seed))
 t_end=time.time()+max(1.0,slot)
 inits=[]
 local_reserve = 0.30*slot if do_local else 0.0
 inits_end = t_end - max(10.0, local_reserve)
 if do_binary and n%2==0 and n>=64 and inits_end-time.time()>20:
    bin_starts=binary_starts(n,rng,2)
    bin_end=min(t_end-5, time.time()+min(slot*0.35, 90.0))
    best_bin=None; best_bin_c=inf
    for bs in bin_starts:
       if time.time()>=bin_end: break
       per=min(60, bin_end-time.time())
       hh,cc=binary_anneal_posneg(bs,n,time.time()+per,seed+1000)
       if hh is not None and cc<best_bin_c: best_bin=hh; best_bin_c=cc
    if best_bin is not None: add_unique_inits(inits, best_bin, 0.0)
 # other starts
 others=build_inits_for_n(n,parent,best_h,best_n,rng,include_structured=True)
 for o in others:
    add_unique_inits(inits, o, 1e-4)
 inits=inits[:max(1,limit)]
 out_h=None; out_c=inf
 for j,h0 in enumerate(inits):
    if time.time() >= inits_end - 2: break
    rem=inits_end-time.time()
    per=rem/float(len(inits)-j)
    per=min(80.0 if n>200 else 90.0, per, rem)
    per=max(12.0, per)
    hh,cc=polish_candidate(h0,n,time.time()+per,seed+2000+7*j,do_local=False)
    if hh is not None and cc<out_c: out_h=hh.copy(); out_c=cc
 if out_h is None:
    # fallback first init or best interp
    h0 = inits[0] if inits else project_feasible(np.full(n,0.5),n)
    out_h=project_feasible(h0,n); out_c=compute_c5(out_h)
 if do_local:
    if time.time() < t_end - 10:
       hh,cc=local_pair_posneg_search(out_h,n,t_end-1,seed+987654)
       if cc<out_c: out_h=hh; out_c=cc
 return out_h,out_c
```

### `binary_starts`
```
def binary_starts(n,rng,count=3):
 out=[]
 structs=structured_starts(n,rng,count=max(6,count))
 for s in structs:
   if len(out)>=count: break
   add_unique_inits(out,s,1e-4)
 # random continuous
 out.append(project_feasible(rng.rand(n)*1.2,n))
 # random binary exact
 h=np.zeros(n); perm=rng.permutation(n); h[perm[:n//2]]=1; add_unique
 return out[:count]
```
Could for binary we want starts that threshold well; structured includes Thue-Morse. Good.

Potential code snippets:

`project_feasible` exact adjust:
```
    diff = target - float(np.sum(out))
    if abs(diff) > 1e-15 and out.size > 0:
        if diff > 0.0:
            idx = int(np.argmax(1.0 - out))
            add = min(diff, 1.0 - out[idx])
        else:
            idx = int(np.argmin(out))
            add = max(diff, -out[idx])
        out[idx] = float(out[idx]) + add
        diff = target - float(np.sum(out))
        if abs(diff) > 1e-15 and out.size > 1:
            if diff > 0.0:
                mask = out < 1.0 - 1e-15
            else:
                mask = out > 1e-15
            cnt = int(np.count_nonzero(mask))
            if cnt > 0:
                out[mask] += diff / float(cnt)
                out = np.clip(out, 0.0, 1.0)
```
Good.

`finalize_feasible` similar but less aggressive.

`func current_c_from_posneg` maybe if arrays empty? n>=1.

`update_pair_posneg`: when n=1, m=0. Code:
```
 dx=2.0/float(n)
 dq0=0.0
 if di !=0: dq0 += di*(2.0*h[i]+di)
 if dj !=0: dq0 += dj*(2.0*h[j]+dj)
 pos[0] -= dq0*dx; neg[0]=pos[0]
 return
```
If i==j (not), cross issue; no.

In vector update, after `pos[1:] += t1[:m]`, `pos[0]` update. For n=1, pos[1:] empty.

`ensure_pq_work` allocation for m=0? `m=max(1,n-1)`; but update for n=1 returns before ensure? It may call ensure if m<=0? We'll check before. Good.


`unique_init_list`:
```
def unique_init_list(inits, tol=1e-5):
 out=[]
 for h,c in inits:
   dup=False
   for oh,oc in out:
     if oh.size==h.size and np.allclose(h,oh,rtol=0,atol=tol):
       dup=True; break
   if not dup: out.append((h,c))
 return out
```

`sort_init_list`:
```
def sort_init_list(inits):
 m=len(inits)
 for i in range(m):
   minj=i
   for j in range(i+1,m):
     if inits[j][1] < inits[i][1]: minj=j
   if minj!=i:
     inits[i],inits[minj]=inits[minj],inits[i]
 return inits
```
No lambda.

```
def polish_candidate(h0,n,slot,seed,do_local=False):
 h=project_feasible(h0,n)
 c=compute_c5(h)
 best_h=h.copy(); best_c=c; cur_h=h.copy(); cur_c=c
 if 2 <= n <= 320 and time.time() < slot - 25.0:
   betas=smooth_betas_for_n(n)
   maxit = 35 if n > 220 else 45
   for beta in betas:
     if time.time() > slot - 20.0: break
     h2,c2=optimize_smooth_h(cur_h,n,beta,maxiter=maxit,ftol=1e-10)
     if c2 < best_c:
       best_h=h2.copy(); best_c=c2
       cur_h=h2.copy(); cur_c=c2
     elif c2 <= cur_c + 1e-6:
       cur_h=h2.copy(); cur_c=c2
     else:
       cur_h=best_h.copy(); cur_c=best_c

   maxit=35 if n>220 else 50
   h2,c2=refine_active(best_h,n,slot,seq,maxiter=maxit,margin=2e-5)
   if c2 < best_c:
     best_h=h2.copy(); best_c=c2
 if do_local and time.time() < slot - 2.0:
   h2,c2=local_pair_posneg_search(best_h,n,slot,seed+31)
   if c2 < best_c: best_h=h2.copy(); best_c=c2
 return best_h,float(best_c)
```

### `choose_active_lags`
```
def choose_active_lags(vals,lags,max_count,margin=2e-5):
 if vals.size==0: return []
 order=np.argsort(-vals)
 take=min(int(max_count), vals.size)
 idx=order[:take]
 if margin>0:
   top=float(vals[order[0]])
   near=np.where(vals >= top - margin)[0]
   idx=np.unique(np.concatenate((idx,near)))
   idx=idx[np.argsort(-vals[idx])]
   cap=min(int(vals.size), max(int(max_count)*2, 80), 130)
   if idx.size > cap: idx=idx[:cap]
 return [int(lags[i]) for i in idx]
```
For n=160, max_count=70, cap 130. Good.
